[9/9/2024 8:45 AM] jyoung8607
My git-fu is reasonably adequate and I don't mind them, they have their value and their place


[9/9/2024 8:47 AM] jyoung8607
In this case, I'm less concerned about the submodule pointers themselves and more concerned about workflows between the various projects


[9/9/2024 8:53 AM] jyoung8607
The new factoring of car code into opendbc enables a lot of really good things, like allowing opendbc to iterate really quickly, with much less of a learning curve for new contributors, and much clearer intended API boundaries, and lower assurance risk for merging changes (don't need nearly as much CI)


[9/9/2024 8:56 AM] jyoung8607
That allows openpilot itself to mature and stabilize in the areas it should, and rapidly iterate in the areas where it needs to, without everyone having *constant* merge conflicts to deal with


[9/9/2024 9:04 AM] jyoung8607
More people can work productively in parallel, and there's more flexibility to let external contributors commit directly in their area of expertise.


[9/9/2024 9:10 AM] jyoung8607
The issue I was commenting about isn't submodules per-se, but the fact that opendbc and panda still have to move in lockstep for any change with safety relevance


[9/9/2024 9:17 AM] jyoung8607
This PR is a good example. Relatively trivial change, but safety relevant. In the old world, it requires synchronized merges in two projects. In the new world, it now requires synchronized merges in THREE projects.
https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/1198

{Embed}
https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/1198
Subaru: driver torque sensor Nm scaling factor and unit by martinl ...
This PR adds Nm unit for Subaru driver torque sensor signal for gen1 and gen2 and updates carstate to use new signal. Matched with SSM4 signal output by to jnewb1 at martinl/openpilot#80
https://images-ext-1.discordapp.net/external/lKXAXp-RNBlg2zeR3yaJcgHNRhn6ukDE9Jzfn-h7V9c/https/opengraph.githubassets.com/6c53cd77cd9add145800ba3bb3285f30d237912d95f312c78012d486b277c3c0/commaai/opendbc/pull/1198

Question then is how the full image gets built and signed and flashed. Not sure of answers here, just thinking.

[9/18/2024 2:52 PM] sshane_
Anyone have any thoughts or preferences about how we manage the car logging and data strucutures? For context, we now duplicate car.capnp in opendbc with dataclasses which is fragile as any changes should be made manually to both. I'm thinking we should either move car.capnp to opendbc and use that as the data structure, or write a converter from one to the other which is automatically committed.

1. Use capnp: we lose the nice static type analysis Python dataclasses give us (requires runtime type checking)
2. Write a converter from capnp to dataclasses: extra work maintaining this converter and requires a dependency on openpilot

https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/1251/files#diff-07b07ba67bfd4fa2389073a0b637eef777225454e2b71bf0d47b4c4bf31d013f


[9/18/2024 3:03 PM] jyoung8607
3. Speedbump on the openpilot side that prevents opendbc submodule bump unless they're in sync? No cross-dependencies, similar situation as CARS.md.


[9/18/2024 3:05 PM] jyoung8607
Basically 2, except running on the openpilot side instead of the opendbc side


[9/18/2024 3:05 PM] jyoung8607
I do like the type checking


[9/18/2024 3:17 PM] adeebshihadeh
I'm pro moving car.capnp, but onroadEvents will be tricky

[9/24/2024 6:30 PM] jyoung8607
@sunnyhaibin have reasonably good confidence that all HKG CAN-FD EV can use the same gear position message. That applies to the six currently upstreamed vehicles plus opendbc#1131. That means we don't need the CANFD_ALT_GEARS_3 flag and logic, we just need to test for CANFD plus EV and always use ACCELERATOR for gear position in that case.

By "reasonably good confidence" I mean the states consistently match up for Drive, and for at least one instance of Park, but instances of Reverse and so forth are pretty rare in commaCarSegments.

Want to tackle a refactor of CarState for that condition? Would probably be good to have some people explicitly test Reverse as well.

https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/pull/33647

{Embed}
https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/pull/33647
tools: notebook for HKG alt message validation by jyoung8607 · Pull...
Jupyter notebook for validating some of the complex HKG alternate message selection logic. As written, it&#39;s meant to look at the gearshift message needed for CAN-FD EVs, but can easily be a...
https://images-ext-1.discordapp.net/external/2JABu8EvUyfQX0Qw5qDJ3yCKNF_O_C5dszxCS4xl-Pc/https/opengraph.githubassets.com/42a6dc9e89bd811704cec43d24af1c0a3806c9b364cccceb24a73c54fda105b0/commaai/openpilot/pull/33647

[10/1/2024 5:26 PM] adeebshihadeh
so that's not quite what I mean. there's a few levels of "support":

**release**: this is the highest level. it shows up on comma.ai/vehicles, we sell everything you need in the shop, works in openpilot release, verified the safety, and we don't expect it to regress

**dashcamOnly**: this is stuff we've merged because it doesn't add complexity to release cars. they have a pathway to release and are in various levels of completion


[10/1/2024 5:26 PM] adeebshihadeh

[10/10/2024 11:42 AM] jyoung8607
depends on how long and detailed you want to get. the openpilot github wiki is one place, but I think comma want that to go away in favor of docs.comma.ai. if it's not super long (say, no more than 10 lines) a comment or docstring within the car port would probably be fine, especially if there's non-obvious logic in the port that needs explanation.

{Embed}
https://www.f150gen14.com/forum/threads/introducing-bluepilot-a-ford-specific-fork-for-comma3x-openpilot.24241/
Introducing Bluepilot! A Ford specific fork for Comma3X / OpenPilot
The goal of bluepilot is to develop, test, and stage Ford specific enhancements, validating them before submission to the SunnyPilot team for inclusion in the parent project.

bluepilot is based on SunnyPilot, and is not intended to compete with or replace SunnyPilot, rather it is a place for...

[10/19/2024 8:47 AM] alesatobrazilsp
There are some cars that Commas cannot test because they will never exist on the US market, and would require a scientific rigor of conviction and a test suite that porters does not have the ability to practice (and perhaps that is why it remains forever in Forkland, "it just works"). As for the country label, a good example is the Corolla, in some countries it is not affected by SecOC. So an international buyer would feel comfortable buying the kit. Another thing that in my personal opinion would be a factor in boosting sales would be to disambiguate what "alpha long" means, maybe I'm wrong because the consumer of a comma product would be quite "geek"?

[10/22/2024 6:39 PM] sshane_
@Torq_boi any reason you made Ford long open loop in this PR? https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/pull/32706

There are some cases where a steady state error is built up after acceleration/deceleration, plus the internal pitch compensation can also wind up and some error correction would help prevent overshoot.

If no real reason, I'll test reverting that (ki=0.5)

[11/7/2024 4:10 PM] debbor
I've started refactoring the 3/Y tesla port a bit to also work with my Model S again (https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/1455), but it's getting quite messy since some of the messages the port relies on are not on the PARTY bus, but rather on the POWERTRAIN/CHASSIS bus instead on the model S.

I think it might make more sense if we could have a single CANParser that handles all the buses at once instead of the cp, cp_cam, cp_body, cp_chassis, cp_adas, ... we have (hardcoded) now in all the car ports.
Thoughts?

{Reactions}
💚

[11/26/2024 4:55 PM] sunnyhaibin
I am working on a PR to parse the **Sport** gear shifter values for Hyundai's EV, HEV, PHEV: https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/pull/34113

I have written a notebook to verify if the targeted platforms have the **Sport** gear signal in the message (sample output in notebook): https://github.com/sunnyhaibin/openpilot-1/blob/hyundai-phev-gear/tools/car_porting/examples/hkg_can_gear_message.ipynb

**Issue:** I have increased the maximum segments to 40 for each platform, and have only identified **one** segment that contains the **Sport** gear logged to the message. Are there any alternative methods to improve the validation for this PR?

We have test routes that some users have confirmed the existence of this signal. However with the big car dataset we are sampling from, the **Sport** gear results are limited.

[11/29/2024 8:23 PM] firstsrif
Is there any way to resend the message with the fault bit updates.. without defining  NEW_SIGNALS like im doing here, makes the dbc so messy and hard to spot new messages

```def block_faults(packer, CAN, enabled, block_faults):
  values = {s: block_faults[s] for s in block_faults.keys()}
  values.update({
    "FAULT_1": 0,
    "FAULT_2": 0,
  })
  return packer.make_can_msg("NEW_MSG_162", CAN.ECAN, values)```

[12/12/2024 8:27 AM] the_great_genie
Hi Nihal, there might be some tweaks you can do to perform a lane change slower, but nothing to make it quicker - without really having a negative effect for the other cases. The lane change is controlled by the model. I spent countless hours to make the lane change smoother for my truck - the only way I was able to achieve that was during lane chance was to lower the change rate, but you can't apply to your case. I would think you can't make the lane change quicker.


[12/12/2024 8:38 AM] nickzz3082
I tried adjusting the steer ratio, but it caused oscillation, making the car go out of bounds. However, setting the steer ratio to 19.4 made the steering slightly faster. As you mentioned, though, there are drawbacks, such as the car swaying and jerking.

had to adjust other parameters also to make it little stable

Vehicle Identification Numbers (VIN codes)/GM/VIN Codes
It is the tenth digit that gives you the model year for GM. I've been working at a Chevy dealer for years running codes/vins. For example (my examples only apply to 2001-2015 gm vehicles)
Remember 10th digit from the beginning, (left to right).
2001... 1, 
2002...2, 
2003...3, 
etc., 
2009...9. 
After 2009, the tenth digit was given a letter
201...

It would be possible to make the car work with op by forcing the car recognition if it uses identical messaging to an already working car, but I highly doubt you are so lucky.

I'd never heard of Luxgen before now and there is little mention of it on this Discord according to a quick search I did.

I think you likely want to start a new topic in the #car-port-projects forum.

The road ahead will likely be pretty bumpy if this is your first project of the sort, but where there's a will, there's a way.

Good luck! 🍀


[1/14/2025 10:50 PM] nworby

[1/19/2025 12:13 AM] dataplex

[1/19/2025 12:42 AM] selorium
First thing is to identify if a car is a good candidate to be ported. If it has adaptive cruise control and lane keep assist, it's likely a good candidate. However there are some recent models which include encrypted CAN network traffic and without a proper encryption key you can't put Openpilot in the middle.

I would read through https://docs.comma.ai/car-porting/what-is-a-car-port/ and watch Jason's Comma Con video about it https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=XxPS5TpTUnI&ab_channel=comma

{Embed}
comma

[1/20/2025 3:04 PM] alesatobrazilsp
run locally: ```
cd ~/openpilot/opendbc_repo && deactivate && source .venv/bin/activate```
(openpilot) changes to (opendbc) , after:
``` uv run pytest -n$(nproc)```
and if u change generator dbc
```scons -j$(nproc) opendbc/dbc``` to produce the _generated version

[1/21/2025 6:36 AM] alesatobrazilsp
take your time a little bit and read the code to understand what this script does. test_model can replay CAN messages from a previous route against your code changes and show the tmux crashes at pytest results.


[1/21/2025 6:38 AM] alesatobrazilsp
For example, if u run `tmux a` and cycle ignition and monitor tmux u will see what is causing systemd crash in your code. With ./test.sh u can perform this without your car and against all other supported cars (in case your changes causing crash/regression to another cars)

{Reactions}
👍

[2/13/2025 6:18 PM] firstsrif
```04CB - memory seats rerlated
0403 - memory seats buttons
0388 - current positions driverseatforwardbackward/rearupdown/reclineupdown (no lumbar and frontupdown memory settings)
04F2 - illumination up/down
0414 - trunk button
04D5 - volumesteer, menuscrollleft scroll left menu, volup/down
0448 - mode, mute, star, voice, seekup/down, selectleftscrollbutton
0488 - headlight stalk
0417 - turn (the stalk) <-- possibly use this for blinker control
0400 - turn (mightbesidecamera)
041A - headlight, highbeammanual
041b - highbeamauto
0422 - wiperstatus
042A - (AC) auto,off,frontdef
0429 - reardef
0449 - heatedsteer
0465 - BEEP AC
0403 - (drivercontrols) windowlock, allwindows, lock/unlock (no mirror controls)
0405 - (passcontrols) lock/unlock (no window)
0411 - doorstatus
04E4 - (Keyfob) trunk, lock, unlock, alarm (no hold or move)```

[3/11/2025 9:23 AM] jayrok94
Is this going to be in 9.8?


[3/11/2025 9:24 AM] jayrok94
@sunnyhaibin  do you know?


[3/11/2025 9:24 AM] sunnyhaibin
Yes and no. They're already merged in `master`, but since Hyundai openpilot longitudinal is **not** in release, it will only work in non-release branches.


[3/11/2025 9:26 AM] sunnyhaibin
So doesn't matter whether it's in 0.9.8 or not, you will still have to use `master` or `nightly-dev` to use it.


[3/11/2025 9:27 AM] jayrok94
Gotcha ok thank you. Is nightly dev, or master more stable then the other?

And for my knowledge what exactly is needed for something like this to make it in an official release.


[3/11/2025 9:28 AM] sunnyhaibin
openpilot longitudinal control for Hyundai/Kia/Genesis is currently still `alpha` quality, so it needs to get out of `alpha` before it can be enabled in release. You can read a bit more of the requirements to get it out of alpha here, someone asked the same yesterday: https://discord.com/channels/469524606043160576/524611978208215070/1348342624846876712


[3/11/2025 9:29 AM] sunnyhaibin
I suggest `nightly-dev`, `master` is **the** bleeding edge branch, and sometimes it may break more than `nightly-dev`.

Thank you for the breakdown, much appreciated!

[3/25/2025 4:11 PM] jyoung8607
MIN_LATERAL_CONTROL_SPEED was 0.3 m/s, default vEgoStopping is 0.5 m/s with only Tesla *substantively* below the former min lateral speed (GM, Rivian, Toyota TSS2 are at 0.25 m/s). below vEgoStopping we're committing to nearly immediate standstill. Any gain here is basically limited to a fraction of a second while *departing* (not entering) standstill.


[3/25/2025 4:11 PM] jyoung8607
i'm down with reducing magic numbers sprinkled in weird places in the stack, if that's what that is, but I struggle to see driving performance gains, at least not immediately


[3/25/2025 4:14 PM] jyoung8607
might be some applicability for stock ACC when creeping super slow


[3/25/2025 4:25 PM] nworby
I imagine that the goal is to remove, or at least greatly simplify, the standstill state machine. If that assumption is correct, then it doesn’t matter what the numbers are.

[5/10/2025 10:39 AM] vanillagorillaaa
It means that the can message ID's (0x48, 0x240, etc.) that openpilot expects to see those messages on, are not present on your car. This could be because they have changed ID's on your car, or are completely missing. Use a cabana to find if/where these messages are at on your car

I've been trying to understand how this all fits together, specifically for modern Ford vehicles (like the Ford Maverick)...
But I'm slightly confused how infomation from the radar sensor is caputred


[5/24/2025 9:10 PM] jjtech
According to the Ford service manuals and what I can gather, Ford no longer directly exposes the HS-CAN, MS-CAN, and I-CAN busses onto the OBD-II port:
Instead, the various CAN busses go to the GWM, which also provides the DIAG1 and DIAG2 busses to the OBD-II port

I presume that the GWM will happily translate CAN messages with OBD-II compliant IDs and broadcast them onto all applicable busses, and I guess it does the same thing for the injected ACC messages sent by the Panda?

But how does the Panda get the GWM to forward it radar messages? Unless the GWM is forwarding all CAN messages onto the DIAG lines?

[6/19/2025 9:27 PM] mooooooooooom
How do I make sure that my changes to opendbc are referenced from my openpilot branch?

Context: i'm trying to add fingerprints etc of a new vehicle, and want to make a test drive with these changes.


[6/20/2025 3:53 PM] mooooooooooom
In a last few hours I came up with this scenario and I am noob with this as well.


1. fork openpilot
2. create new branch in openpilot you will be making your changes in if any
3. opendbc_repo is a submodule, do your changes in it in a separate branch. Once done, leave that repository be (at your latest commit)
4. git status in openpilot repo should show changes in opendbc_repo
5. git add opendbc_repo
6. git commit -m 'my changes'
7. git push

then test on your comma

[7/23/2025 10:51 AM] l7weaver
I'm thinking of building some tooling for letting a LLM try to figure out CAN msgs, but my expectations that the results are going to be no different from brute forcing/random guesses


[7/23/2025 10:58 AM] l7weaver
I think it needs the following for it to do anything productive:
```
- info about the car (make, model, trim, year, engine type)
- a 100% validated DBC file with no unknown/unsure/NEW_MSG signals/msgs (to use as a reference)
- full export of a route as a CSV
- a carefully tailored prompt to be very conservative with guesses and to be skeptical of the previous AI's guesses
- a tool and schema made for testing AI guesses against validated data
- notes for the AI to keep track of
```


[7/23/2025 11:04 AM] l7weaver
Regarding a schema for guesses, could be something like this:
```
{
  "MSG_ID_HERE": {
    "REFERENCE_MSG": ["REF_MSG_ID_HERE"],
    "EXPECTED_MAX_VALUE": "value_here",
    "EXPECTED_MIN_VALUE": "value_here",
    "START_BIT": "start_bit_here",
    "END_BIT": "end_bit_here",
    "FACTOR": "factor_here",
    "OFFSET": "offset_here",
    "DESCRIPTION": "short descrption of the guess, (e.g. this is related to vehicle speed)"
  }
}
```


[7/23/2025 11:11 AM] l7weaver
and that tool would return a few values for the AI to use as a feedback loop
```
- Similarity to validated signal value = 0 to 1 (e.g. 50% would be 0.5)
- ExceededMaxValue = true/false
- ExceededMinValue = true/false
```


[7/23/2025 11:18 AM] adeebshihadeh
here's another way to think about it, disregarding the frequencies:

you have (a) the set of messages that is actually used in `update()` and (b) the set of messages given by `get_can_parsers()`
* when a is a subset of b, it's all good, you just get some extra checks on canValid
* when b is a subset of a, you get a `KeyError` when trying to read
* when they're the same set, nothing changes

[7/23/2025 5:51 PM] jyoung8607
Pseudocode for today:

```
pt_cp = CANParser(powertrain.dbc, [("ENGINE", [frequency optional])], 0)
cam_cp = CANParser(powertrain.dbc, [("CAMERA", [frequency optional])], 2)  # Same DBC for bus we're splitting

update()
  ret.gasPressed = pt_cp.vl["ENGINE"]["GAS_PRESSED"]
  ret.doesStockLkasSuck = cam_cp.vl["CAMERA"]["DOES_STOCK_LKAS_SUCK"]
```

* For correct wiring, works and goes canValid == True on both buses, correct behavior.
* Swap the buses, canValid == False on both buses due to messages not present, correct behavior.

Pseudocode for the dynamic parser:
```
pt_cp = CANParser(powertrain.dbc, 0)
cam_cp = CANParser(powertrain.dbc, 2)  # Same DBC for bus we're splitting

update()
  ret.gasPressed = pt_cp.vl["ENGINE"]["GAS_PRESSED"]
  ret.doesStockLkasSuck = cam_cp.vl["CAMERA"]["DOES_STOCK_LKAS_SUCK"]
```

* For correct wiring, works and goes canValid == True on both buses, correct behavior, and even learns frequencies.
* Swap the buses, if I understand correctly, reads zeroes into each carState signal while canValid remains True because we're getting perfectly valid CAN traffic on both buses, just not the ones we needed. No KeyError due to same DBC. If you changed CANParser to only create`cp.vl` entries dynamically as messages are received (haven't tried to figure that out) then we just crash card on KeyError, or we have to create some new way to deal with KeyError.

[7/28/2025 12:13 PM] davidcaliforniaa
-
Overarching purpose of my `CarDocs` related PRs is to work towards making current `CarDocs` simpler so it can migrate with ease away from `values.py` when the time comes. This then makes adding nonfunctional details to a car easier, and more clear, while not clogging up `values.py`. 

Really want to have more vids of people [removing specifically camera covers](https://discord.com/channels/469524606043160576/1318660916329451540/1394733979722317886) and have better status updates with carports(think Dominos pizza tracker but for carports), but all of that ends up adding noise in `values.py` if we extend `CarDocs` in current form. 

Some more context : Github comment [here](https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/2472#discussion_r2209817263) and also have a #opendbc Metadata Framework  in current-projects

[8/9/2025 4:19 PM] jyoung8607

[8/9/2025 4:25 PM] zeroabilitytopay
2017 RAV4H, I'm trying to chase down engine rpm just as a fun chance to learn more about cabana and CAN in general. I see several unknown values but one caught my interest with it's interesting tendency to settle at zero and then hover in a predictable range then settle at zero again. I was hoping to stream cabana while hooked up to the car and vary my throttle to see if it's actually rpm or not


[8/9/2025 4:36 PM] jyoung8607
Just start the car and rev the engine to some known values in Park. Rev to 1000 for 5 seconds, then 2000 for 5 seconds, then 3000 for 5 seconds, then let it idle back down and shut off. That'll give you a nice clear stair-step signal to look for, without any confounding factors from actual movement or driving.

{Reactions}
❤️

[8/9/2025 4:37 PM] jyoung8607
Then you have all day to look at the log results on your desk.

[8/15/2025 11:04 PM] sunnyhaibin
@jyoung8607 @adeeb re: safety model for new brand ports, I'll open a PR with some links to the active ports that we're looking to support on sunnypilot *sooner* (that'll eventually make their way into openpilot when the path is clear and the OP team's porting velocity opens up)


[8/15/2025 11:06 PM] sunnyhaibin
The MADS logic in our opendbc safety implementation is [pretty self-contained](https://github.com/sunnypilot/opendbc/blob/master/opendbc/safety/sunnypilot/safety_mads.h), so it should be relatively straightforward to upstream with some cleanup. The core changes at the car safety level are minimal - mainly just adding rx_checks for `acc_main_on` and LKAS button detection in each platform's safety mode file. For example, what we do to check Hyundai CAN's LDA (not LKAS) button press for MADS to consume: https://github.com/sunnypilot/opendbc/blob/cf9b33143fb7c1a337be9c3f20428a9fb17323e1/opendbc/safety/modes/hyundai.h#L165-L167

One thing that would really help is having panda publish `controls_allowed_lat` alongside [the existing signals](https://github.com/commaai/panda/blob/5b0f1a2eca710121ef7573f1d183d4bc6c07b6ec/board/main_comms.h#L18). This would let us implement a [`heartbeat_engaged_mads_mismatches` check with MADS](https://github.com/sunnypilot/opendbc/blob/cf9b33143fb7c1a337be9c3f20428a9fb17323e1/opendbc/safety/sunnypilot/safety_mads.h#L133-L142) (which is currently not enabled) similar to what you have for `controls_allowed`, so we could make sure openpilot and opendbc safety stay in sync about lateral control.

We've got [safety tests with a list of edge cases](https://github.com/sunnypilot/opendbc/blob/master/opendbc/safety/tests/mads_common.py) that gets validated on every CI run, probably a bit overkill but they catch a lot of edge cases we've encountered over the years 😊

To my knowledge, other forks aren't using our current MADS implementation. frogpilot still uses ["Always on Lateral"](https://github.com/pfeiferj/pfeifer-pilot-patches/commit/fccd7f77a37707c2034cb36297aad1006b4cb320) (great approach authored by Jacob), which works but doesn't have the same level of safety checks. The main issue with that approach is it could automatically engage lateral when some cars shift into Drive without explicit user entry, which our MADS implementation specifically blocks at the safety level.

Happy to discuss this more in depth and we can put together a PR if this is something we would like to upstream. We could start with a few platforms as POC.

So TLDR for prerequisites of MADS in upstream opendbc safety:
- Alternative experience for MADS
- New field for `pandaState.controls_allowed_lat` so we can start checking `heartbeat_engaged_mads_mismatches` with openpilot

[8/25/2025 1:19 PM] devtekve
TIL you can debug panda safety using breakpoints with replay_drive. You must attach an LLDB debugger after you launch replay_drive (and making sure no optimizations are made and symbols aren't stripped) and there you go. 

```json
{
    "version": "0.2.0",
    "configurations": [
        {
            "name": "Attach LLDB to Python",
            "type": "lldb",
            "request": "attach",
            "pid": "${command:pickMyProcess}",
            "initCommands": [
                "script import time; time.sleep(5)"
            ]
        },
        {
            "name": "Python Launch",
            "type": "python",
            "request": "launch",
            "program": "${workspaceFolder}/opendbc/safety/tests/safety_replay/replay_drive.py",
            "args": [
                "e1107f9d04dfb1e2/0000045c--da2e374469"
            ],
            "console": "integratedTerminal",
            "justMyCode": false,
            "debugOptions": [
                "RedirectOutput"
            ],
            "env": {
                "PYTHONPATH": "${workspaceFolder}"
            },
            "subProcess": true,
            "stopOnEntry": false
        }
    ],
    "compounds": [
        {
            "name": "Python + Native (LLDB)",
            "configurations": [
                "Python Launch",
                "Attach LLDB to Python"
            ]
        }
    ]
}
```

on libsafety's sconscript you add the following CFLAGS 
```
    '-g',
    '-O0',
    "-fno-omit-frame-pointer"
```

https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/1282121586002104402/1409603043053408266/debug-panda-safety.mov?ex=6a625b22&is=6a6109a2&hm=4f9d89c4dff9ab730d50284fc958e12406ee434e60d9078fca9c191f3bb019cf&

[8/25/2025 11:49 PM] sunnyhaibin
It’s always sending checksum and counter whether engaged/disengaged, otherwise the car will fault, most cars we block the source message from the camera, and we send our own via tx.

Most brands do not stop blocking, instead they send their own via carcontroller all the time

{Reactions}
👍

[8/25/2025 11:52 PM] sunnyhaibin
You can see how all of them flow with rx/tx in `opendbc/safety/safety.h`


[8/26/2025 2:20 AM] devtekve
@shane I think I've found an issue with the current angle steering safety though. 

This doesn't seem to happen on tesla because this only really happens when a human override is in effect, and tesla pretty much disengages upon human override (*correct me if im wrong*)

The timeline of what happens is:

0. Last angle was: `-32.736°` while the driver was overriding (this drops `lat_active` to false) https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/blob/master/opendbc/car/lateral.py#L124-L125
1. Model / controller requests `-32.841°`.
2. With a jerk limit at that time of `0.283°` we end up with a Jerk-limited change to: `-34.517°`.
3.  With a Max angle due to max lat accel applied of: `-28.302°`.
4. **Final angle steering = `-28.302°`.**

This is blocked by panda safety, because panda safety wants both jerk **and** max lat angle to be fulfilled, otherwise it's a fault. But in this case, the final angle steering is too distant from the last angle (`-32.736°`), causing it to break the `jerk` limits.

I have a feeling we should prioritize jerk over lat accel, since a jerk it's too sudden, while lat accel tends to be progressive.


[8/26/2025 9:46 AM] edawg2786

[9/11/2025 9:44 AM] smasher816
So @GreenCap has started parsing the toyota contental radar message format ([here](https://discord.com/channels/469524606043160576/1195598390109683762)). Each can message with 0x80 addr attached is only 8 bytes long (1 byte header, 7 byte payload).
However, for the complete radar state you need to collect and concatenate 106 messages together to get a full 848 byte message (with 742 byte payload).
```
742 bytes {
  0xE4,
  16 bytes {
    ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??,
  },
  36 bytes {
    ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, ??, 0x00
  }[20],
  4 bytes {
    ??, ??, ??, ??,
  }
  0x00
}
```

Is there a dbc syntax to select bits crossing a packet boundry? Or would this have to be handled in a custom python implementation higher upstream?
i.e. imagine this theoretical dbc class
```
BO_ 128 MyBigRadarMessage
  SG_ MessageID : 2 bytes
  SG_ Header: 16 bytes
  SG_ Object_0: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_1: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_2: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_3: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_4: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_5: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_6: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_7: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_8: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_9: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_10: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_11: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_12: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_13: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_14: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_15: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_16: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_17: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_18: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_19: 36 bytes
  SG_ Object_20: 36 bytes
  SG_ Footer: 4 bytes
```

and this radar_interface code
```
for i in range(0, 20):
  print(i, self.rcp.vl["MyBigRadarMessage"][f"Object_{i}"]["XXX"])
```

[9/11/2025 4:57 PM] l7weaver
If we assume passenger weight is GVWR - curb weight, then we get 430kg


[9/11/2025 4:57 PM] l7weaver
then we divide passenger weight by half, we get 215kg


[9/11/2025 4:58 PM] l7weaver

[9/11/2025 4:58 PM] l7weaver
Front axle curb weight = 848kg (1063 - 215)


[9/11/2025 4:59 PM] l7weaver
Rear axle curb weight = 787kg (1002 - 215)


[9/11/2025 5:12 PM] l7weaver

[9/12/2025 1:33 AM] sctivcrmn
Hi all, I'm running into an issue with OpenDBC. 
BMW FlexRay uses a lot of multiplexing, and almost every message is multiplexed.
The OpenDBC parser doesn’t appear to support demultiplexing, parser's `update` function will overrides cp.vl[msg], in the worst case, a signal will never be fetched into carState.
I want to add support for demultiplexing, which part would be suitable for modification?
1. Mix the demux value into the address, e.g. `⁠address << 8 | demux_value`. This would extend the address space and separate the signals into different keys.
2. Modify the ⁠cp.vl structure by adding a new bucket to separate muxed signals.


[9/12/2025 2:55 AM] sctivcrmn
It doesn’t seem to be a problem, I can use cp.vl_all.

[9/27/2025 8:59 AM] alesatobrazilsp
<http://repo.darmajaya.ac.id/4749/1/The%20Car%20Hacker%E2%80%99s%20Handbook_%20A%20Guide%20for%20the%20Penetration%20Tester%20%28%20PDFDrive%20%29.pdf>


[9/27/2025 9:00 AM] alesatobrazilsp


{Attachments}
https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/1282121586002104402/1421496833154355323/image.png?ex=6a621e97&is=6a60cd17&hm=d69fb0e0418fa463c89624613227be4c8a7490e143a939bd12842bbfb1e58458&


[9/27/2025 9:04 AM] alesatobrazilsp
Read this book. In the diagram, find out who the decision-making master is and where they are located. Find out where the slave who obeys the "EPS" commands is located, and where they are located. Look for proprietary information on how communication between them occurs (CRC, Checksum...)


[9/27/2025 9:05 AM] alesatobrazilsp
Find the wiring harness that goes to the EPS on the steering column. Find the CAN wire pair in this harness. Determine which is low and which is high:


[9/27/2025 9:07 AM] alesatobrazilsp
1.5 ~ 2.5V is CAN Low 
2.7 ~ 3.5V is CAN High


[9/27/2025 9:09 AM] alesatobrazilsp
In the Development wire harness from comma.ai, find the green and orange wires. Green is CAN-L and orange is CAN-H. Place the steering side on bus2 and the rest of the car (decision maker) side on bus0.


[9/27/2025 9:15 AM] alesatobrazilsp
read this to understand how DBC files (CAN high-level dictonary) works: https://medium.com/@energee/what-are-dbc-files-469a3bf9b04b

{Embed}
https://medium.com/@energee/what-are-dbc-files-469a3bf9b04b
What are DBC files
A DBC file describes a car’s network traffic, what modules are on it, and their messages and signals.
https://images-ext-1.discordapp.net/external/vJwVg3ow3aYXXTx_qRoAVHOU3qXJswvxYedpA6EwKnA/https/miro.medium.com/v2/da%3Atrue/resize%3Afit%3A300/1%2AsXicdoHYAvvcoqlT24BiJw.gif


[9/27/2025 9:21 AM] alesatobrazilsp
After these (phew, many steps), you'll need to understand how the safety model works and its reasons for existing. The main reason is to provide the driver with the opportunity to defend themselves (with sufficient time and speed) to intercept a very wrong command. To do this, you'll need to understand what safety hooks do in your code. Write tests that limit the action to safe levels, and have fun. If you're frustrated, take a breath and do something else. Come back only when it's fun.

To see data read about openpilot/tools

[10/10/2025 1:04 PM] sunnyhaibin
That's some good bit of faults


[10/10/2025 1:07 PM] sunnyhaibin
From the few faults they happen when steering angle is above 90 degrees, which is possibly something due to the internal temp fault that cuts steering due to high steering angle. Stock openpilot and other forks have this bypassed, but sometimes it doesn't work for some cars.

Other instances of faults triggered when it was going straight, no clue what those could be.

[10/17/2025 11:28 AM] alesatobrazilsp
Nothing prevents you from making a customization in the interface, if you use the lateral alternative experience. In the longitudinal it disables instantly respecting safety completely. On the side, the MADS is taken advantage of to create a smooth lateral deactivation transition touching only in car interface code and nothing in safety (take advantage of MADS). This is something very good that Chinese domestic lane centering already has. I think it's a great Chinese victory


[10/17/2025 11:30 AM] alesatobrazilsp
The ADAS systems of the Chinese domestic luxury car market are at a much more advanced level than the rest of the world


[10/17/2025 11:31 AM] alesatobrazilsp
much much more


[10/17/2025 11:34 AM] alesatobrazilsp

[10/23/2025 1:03 PM] jyoung8607
steeringTorqueEps traditionally contains the EPS motor output. It's not required for openpilot unless you have an EPS like Toyota where we need to know it to avoid faulting the EPS


[10/23/2025 1:04 PM] jyoung8607
In short, feel free to ignore steeringTorqueEps unless and until you find a specific need


[10/23/2025 1:05 PM] jyoung8607
it's to avoid faulting the Toyota EPS


[10/23/2025 1:08 PM] cristianku

steeringTorqueEps doesn't really come from a sensor, it's the EPS basically reporting its current power output

[10/23/2025 6:56 PM] jacobwaller
and my safety code is fairly simple as well:
```
  if (msg->addr == MSG_SUBARU_ES_LKAS_ANGLE) {
    int desired_angle = GET_BYTES(msg, 5, 3) & 0x1FFFFU;
    desired_angle = -1 * to_signed(desired_angle, 17);
    bool lkas_request = GET_BIT(msg, 12U);

    violation |= steer_angle_cmd_checks(desired_angle, lkas_request, SUBARU_ANGLE_STEERING_LIMITS);
  }
```

[10/26/2025 7:29 PM] jacobwaller
just in case your next question is "what is the car controller doing?"
```
    if (self.frame % self.p.STEER_STEP) == 0:
      if self.CP.flags & SubaruFlags.LKAS_ANGLE:
        actual_steering_angle_deg = CS.out.steeringAngleDeg
        desired_steering_angle_deg = actuators.steeringAngleDeg

        apply_steer = apply_std_steer_angle_limits(
          desired_steering_angle_deg,
          self.apply_steer_last,
          CS.out.vEgoRaw,
          actual_steering_angle_deg,
          CC.latActive,
          self.p.ANGLE_LIMITS
        )

        if not CC.latActive:
          apply_steer = actual_steering_angle_deg

        can_sends.append(subarucan.create_steering_control_angle(self.packer, apply_steer, CC.latActive))
        self.apply_steer_last = apply_steer
      else: 
```

limits are defined in CarControllerParams:
```
    unrestricted_angle_limits: AngleSteeringLimits = AngleSteeringLimits(
        100,
        ([0., 5., 35.], [5., .8, .15,]),
        ([0., 5., 35.], [5., .8, .15,]),
    )

    self.ANGLE_LIMITS = make_limits_slightly_more_restrictive(unrestricted_angle_limits)
```

that `make_limits_slightly_more_restrictive` literally just multiplies all the angle rates by 0.99. I was testing to see if it was floating point math causing issues (it wasn't)

[10/26/2025 8:41 PM] jyoung8607
your max steer angle in panda is 545 degrees, max steer angle in openpilot is 100 degrees. ordinarily you'd think having panda be "more" permissive would be good, but when you went `latActive` off standstill with an already-sharp real steering angle, commanding the angle to -100 degrees (clipped by apply_std_steer_angle_limits based on openpilot's limits) is a 46.1 degree instant jump from its current position

[10/29/2025 3:05 PM] jyoung8607
This code structure is a problem -- the way it's written, it's going read steering angles from BOTH `Steering_2` (intended for angle-steering cars) and `Steering_Torque` (intended for torque steering cars). In your car's logs, `Steering_Torque` is present but the angle signal is always zero. Both messages are received at 50Hz, so half of your angle samples will be exactly 0.0 degrees.
https://github.com/jacobwaller/opendbc/blob/62fa9c4e640b2e04919851a7945891656dbf04fd/opendbc/safety/modes/subaru.h#L107-L124

{Embed}
https://github.com/jacobwaller/opendbc/blob/62fa9c4e640b2e04919851a7945891656dbf04fd/opendbc/safety/modes/subaru.h
opendbc/opendbc/safety/modes/subaru.h at 62fa9c4e640b2e04919851a794...
a Python API for your car. Contribute to jacobwaller/opendbc development by creating an account on GitHub.
https://images-ext-1.discordapp.net/external/vfgaHFMEGrQJcDuk4folN5D4yyRyh1XTorL0uH7jzQw/https/opengraph.githubassets.com/a675f0de4b53a44b5250fb997c71413c481d76ecdd8fda77f685464c151ce8cc/jacobwaller/opendbc

[11/4/2025 4:27 AM] loetkolben
I wanted to bring up issue [#2766](https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/issues/2766#issuecomment-3485026625) regarding blocked TX messages during gas override again.
Some cars (Rivian, Tesla, VW (MEB), Honda) fault ACC or act unpredictably when TX messages are blocked during gas override.

Why does this blocking logic even exist? The stock ACC on Rivian and Tesla keep sending continuous accel commands even when the gas is pressed.
Can we disable this check for specific cars?
Otherwise, we could modify the safety logic to only block messages when accel != 0 for two consecutive frames during gas override. This gives card one frame to react and should eliminate most blocked messages while keeping the safety logic.

{Reactions}
👍 (2)

[11/15/2025 3:07 PM] alesatobrazilsp
```c

void setup() {
  Serial.begin(9600);
  pinMode(7, INPUT_PULLUP);
  pinMode(LED_BUILTIN, OUTPUT);
  pinMode(8, OUTPUT);
}

void loop() {
  digitalWrite(LED_BUILTIN, HIGH);
  Serial.println("BadContact Test");
  digitalWrite(8, LOW);
  if (digitalRead(7) == HIGH) {
    Serial.println("Bad Contact Happened");
    digitalWrite(LED_BUILTIN, LOW);
    while(true) {}
  }
  Serial.println(("BadContact Testing"));
}
```


[11/15/2025 3:10 PM] alesatobrazilsp
When building a wiring harness, a poor electrical connection can cause failures in your car's dashboard (DTC). Simply testing continuity with a multimeter may not be enough. For a more reliable test, crimp terminals and solder the wires to an Arduino to check each cable individually.


[11/15/2025 3:12 PM] alesatobrazilsp
**When running the test, be sure to flex and wiggle the wires, applying special pressure near the connectors.**

Here's why this is a critical step:

Finding the Hidden Flaw: A wire may show perfect continuity when lying still but fail the moment it is moved. The break is often hidden inside the insulation or at the connector's crimp point, where stress is greatest.

Simulating Real-World Conditions: This movement replicates the vibration, bending, and temperature changes the harness will experience inside a vehicle, helping you catch intermittent failures that a static test would miss.

Optimized Testing Method:

Secure the Harness: Anchor the main part of the harness so movement is isolated to the areas you are testing.

Test and Move: While the Arduino is monitoring the circuit, gently bend, twist, and tug each wire along its length.

Focus on Stress Points: Pay extra attention to the areas right behind the terminals and connectors—this is where wires most commonly break.

Observe the Results: Your Arduino test rig should instantly log any momentary signal loss or change in resistance, identifying the exact faulty wire and location.


[11/15/2025 3:37 PM] alesatobrazilsp

[11/15/2025 3:50 PM] alesatobrazilsp
This is for when you're building your first prototype and porting it to your own vehicle, so testing each cable individually is feasible. However, if you're selling to friends in your country, it's more worthwhile to get a male Molex connector and test them all at once, like the [Comma hacker challenge](https://github.com/commaai/harness_tester_challenge), and then submit a harness pull request to Comma to have it included in the kit's sales.

[1/21/2026 3:06 PM] jyoung8607
Ideally you would add support to the DBC parser/packer itself, but not all car ports have done that. As an easier path, here's two examples of car ports that also compose their checksum inside brandcan.py, much as you're doing. You just call the packer without the checksum populated, then call it again with the calculated checksum set.

[1/23/2026 9:48 AM] davidcaliforniaa
```python
opendbc/car/docs_definitions.py

@dataclass
class CarDocs:
  # make + model + model years
  name: str

  # the simplest description of the requirements for the US market
  package: str
```

Oh I see. Before, I was confused why stating the package(HDA II) twice in `name` and also in `package` was needed until I ran into the store link which uses `https://comma.ai/shop/comma-3x?harness={self.name}`. 

It looks like the package is added to `name` for certain models to make sure the name is unique to get the correct harness(as u mentioned). The tradeoff is now there is requirement/package information in `name` when `package` was previously specifically meant for that

[2/10/2026 4:10 PM] sunnyhaibin
Some reasons (that comma staff have mentioned previously):
1. Not all cars are equal. Some cars/models/brands would not work from the location(s) that the comma device intercepts or interacts with. Way too many variants to implement and validate.
2. It does not help them solve self-driving cars, so inherently these features are on a much lower-priority comparing to others.

[2/13/2026 8:47 AM] mvl3c
@RickSticks you need to relink your submodule.   Editing gitmodules alone won't work.   9 in 10 web or AI search replies are useless, so to save you time here's what I do: I store scripts like below in notepad and then paste them into my wsl command line whenever I want to refresh a submodule:

```git fetch --all
git switch 0103-op-honda
git pull
git submodule deinit -f opendbc_repo
git rm -f opendbc_repo
rm -rf .git/modules/opendbc_repo
git submodule add -b combined-honda-new https://github.com/mvl-boston/opendbc opendbc_repo
git commit -m "repointing opendbc_repo"
git remote set-url origin git@github.com:mvl-boston/openpilot.git
git push origin HEAD:0103-op-honda```

[2/16/2026 6:27 PM] l7weaver
so I wrote a quick test


[2/16/2026 6:28 PM] l7weaver
`181 failed, 93 passed in 27.91s`


[2/16/2026 6:28 PM] l7weaver
here's the test


[2/16/2026 6:28 PM] l7weaver
```py
@pytest.mark.parametrize("route", routes)
def test_routes_are_public(route):
  """Verify that all test routes are publicly accessible via the API"""
  api_host = "https://api.commadotai.com"
  canonical_route = route.route.replace('/', '|')
  url = f"{api_host}/v1/route/{canonical_route}/files"

  # Make request without authentication to verify route is public
  resp = requests.get(url, timeout=30)
  assert resp.status_code == 200, \
    f"{route.route} ({route.car_model}) is not public."
```

[2/18/2026 3:51 PM] ricksticks.
For anyone new and following my post on adding Fingerprinting to solve **“Car not recognized”**, there is a procedure missing in the **Fingerprinting doc** (https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/wiki/Fingerprinting) at [**Step 4: Add new firmware versions**].

{Attachments}

[2/18/2026 3:56 PM] ricksticks.
My message.txt got cutoff, here is the rest.    After you add firmware to fingerprints.py, you need to relink your submodule opendbc_repo for the changes to take effect. You have to use Github command line to execute the linking script, in my case I did it on my Windows pc by downloading GIT, at https://git-scm.com/install/windows.     Thanks to @MVL for his help.

More recently, the panda is able to connect to the car and allows me to send steering input, but the car does not respond to my throttle/ braking input. Has anybody run into this before?


[2/25/2026 12:51 PM] jyoung8607
You're correct that we weren't seeing much from the radar. For that segment-range, we were only occasionally seeing a radar lead when vision could see one pretty consistently. Right now it's somewhat expected for longitudinal planning to be less effective with noisy vision-only leads.

We should probably have raised `radarUnavailableTemporary` here, and we didn't. This not being an upstream supported car, I'll leave finding a signal for that up to you.

As another layer on top, conformance with openpilot's longitudinal plan isn't great. From a controls perspective, the aggressive integral config is the only reason it kind of works, and I think there's further tampering at the car port level. I haven't looked into long on any of the old Nidec cars before, so maybe this is how they all are. This is why I wanted to spend some time looking at a better feedforward function for the cars that need torque demand to accelerate.

[3/11/2026 2:50 PM] jyoung8607
At a glance, that looks like a step back. Prior state was no generated artifacts in git, no change there, they were generated at build time. New state is to generate all DBCs at startup, in-memory. You can still call the generator directly to save artifacts locally on-disk, but neither I nor Copilot can find a place that's currently called.

Untried, visual review only, but default state APPEARS to be:
* cabana and plotjuggler won't have DBCs for cars that rely on the generator
* We now pay the full generator CPU and wall-clock time cost at every single startup, including on-device
* We now cache every generated DBC in-memory, not just the car we care about
* I *think* both `card` and `selfdrived` pay this cost independently unless launched by manager

Am I not getting something?


[3/11/2026 4:33 PM] adeebshihadeh
i don't get what you're saying? you don't like it's different?


[3/11/2026 4:33 PM] adeebshihadeh
you say there is a cost, what is it?


[3/11/2026 4:49 PM] jyoung8607
As far as I can tell, that PR inserts the equivalent of running the generator at every single startup. Yeah you're skipping the writes to disk, but you're doing all the reads and spending all the CPU.

(taken on C4 without that change, haven't measured with)
```comma@comma-1b4de818:/data/openpilot$ time opendbc/dbc/generator/generator.py 

real    0m1.564s
user    0m0.770s
sys    0m0.470s
```


[3/11/2026 4:50 PM] adeebshihadeh
well yes, that's the point


[3/11/2026 4:50 PM] jyoung8607
Well, you are obsessive (admirably and correctly) about hammering down startup time, and that just added startup time


[3/11/2026 4:50 PM] adeebshihadeh
btw i'm not asking for first pass concerns


[3/11/2026 4:50 PM] adeebshihadeh
i'm asking for real concrete things


[3/11/2026 4:51 PM] adeebshihadeh
for example, my only real concern with this is that the full generated DBC isn't as easy to access anymore


[3/11/2026 4:52 PM] adeebshihadeh

[3/11/2026 4:52 PM] jyoung8607

[4/3/2026 5:36 AM] alesatobrazilsp
The opendbc has to be compatible with the panda submodule (the safety and the electronic firmware, such as the CAN message structure, the safety package version, the socketcan that speaks via SPI or USB in the case of comma three ...) and also with the openpilot (how it compiles, dependency versions). I usually work like this I have a frozen master version for tester with comma three and version synchronized with the current master for testers with comma four and threex. Both openpilot + panda, and opendbc.


[4/3/2026 5:40 AM] alesatobrazilsp
And yes, you can edit the code directly on the comma device as long as it is in a non-release branch. Don't forget ```echo -en "1" > /data/params/d/DisableUpdates``` so that the updater disappears with your work. You can use nano, vin via ssh to start and then use some IDE like vscode for example that will edit the code directly inside the comma with the ssh add-in installed in vscode.

[4/3/2026 8:15 PM] hiimisaac

[4/3/2026 8:16 PM] jyoung8607
What's the new way?


[4/3/2026 8:18 PM] hiimisaac
Same can message, different signal. 

The current upstream is curvature, which isn’t stateless. It aggregates messages and stacks in the pscm controller. 

The other message, path angle, is stateless and so we can steer and unwind in ways we couldn’t before. Curvatures lining up nearly 1:1 from the model. 

It’s basically a single replacement line for actual steering, and maybe some smoothing due to model noise.


[4/3/2026 8:18 PM] jyoung8607

[4/4/2026 12:48 AM] .icedmoca
Hey, I’m new here, I've been following OpenDBC for a while.

I’ve been working on a system that attempts to automatically generate DBC mappings from live CAN traffic and wanted to sanity check the approach with people here.

My pipeline:
```
Passive CAN capture (no injection)
Frame normalization + rolling buffer per arbitration ID
Byte-level change detection + entropy/range analysis
Correlation against known ground-truth signals (e.g. OBD Mode 01 RPM, speed, throttle)
Candidate signal extraction (byte index + scaling hypotheses)
Confidence scoring based on consistency + cross-sample validation
Optional assisted labeling for ambiguous signals
```
Goal is basically:

* reduce manual reverse engineering
* bootstrap DBCs for unknown vehicles
* then refine over time with more driving data

Right now it outputs candidate mappings like:

CAN ID + byte offset
inferred signal type (linear/boolean/stepped)
estimated scale/offset
confidence score

I’m not claiming it replaces manual validation, but it gets you a strong starting point quickly.

has anyone here tried something similar?
what are the biggest failure cases you’ve seen when correlating CAN = OBD signals?
any gotchas around multi signal packing or multiplexing I should account for early?


[4/4/2026 5:37 AM] tealv2
given how widely varying CAN signals can be this idea has been presented here by someone every few at this point 😆


[4/4/2026 5:37 AM] tealv2
if you want to, make one


[4/4/2026 6:44 AM] .icedmoca
Ok! I currently have bridged raw CAN data to interpreted signals to executable control logic in one.

Currently:
User taps record then performs the action in the car then the system captures the CAN sequence then the user saves it as a named control and then the button appears in the (app) to replay it.

About the multiplexing, I implicitly handle it by recording full frame sequences tied to a real action, not static byte meanings. If a mux changes layout, it just treats it as different states in the sequence, so replay still works because I captured the exact pattern under that condition. For counters, checksums, and rolling codes I avoid false signals by observing which bytes change deterministically vs with the action. Replaying entire valid sequences (including counters and checksums) instead of isolating the bytes. So the integrity fields stay valid without needing to reverse engineer them. 

I'm using the rpm, speed, throttle as grounding and validation, but for controls like lights, doors, etc.. I use direct CAN behavior capture not OBD correlation. Then to bypass signal packing and odd formats I don't need bit decoding up front,
treating frames as opaque packets that produce an effect.. Also, it learns per each vehicle behavior from live capture with no hardcoded assumptions about layout or protocol. Then to reduce the noise or false positives I've been trying to tie signals to real actions using repeatability, same action to the same sequence. And the confidence scoring is based on consistency.


[4/4/2026 7:08 AM] alesatobrazilsp

[4/4/2026 8:30 AM] .icedmoca
@alesato  I integrated it into execution.

The system:
```
Classifies frames via entropy + correlation + counter analysis
Identifies MAC regions and locks them during mutation and replay
Routes SecOC frames through timing strategies instead of naive replay
Automatically discovers alternative nonsecoc control paths via active probing + state validation

```
Secoc isn’t much of a  blocker anymore, it’s a routing signal in the control pipeline.


[4/4/2026 8:32 AM] alesatobrazilsp
https://discord.com/channels/469524606043160576/539127103853953038/1246533878202765422


[4/4/2026 8:41 AM] .icedmoca
@alesato ok ill shutup until i show you a video of me doing it in 3 trys:

```
CAN ID: 0x2F1
classification:
  byte 0-3: payload (low entropy, correlated)
  byte 4: counter (monotonic increment, wraps at 0xF)
  byte 5-7: MAC (high entropy, low correlation, trailing bias)

entropy:
  [1.2, 1.5, 1.3, 1.1, 2.8, 4.7, 4.9, 4.8]

avg correlation:
  payload: ~0.6
  MAC: ~0.05

classification confidence: 0.91
securityStrength: strong

```

[4/15/2026 12:44 PM] alvazz
I’m planning to port my vehicle, but I ran into a question while trying to create the wiring harness. I’ve been trying to understand the information described in hardware/harness/v1/open_pinout.sch.pdf. I’d really appreciate it if anyone could help clarify the following points for me.
1: What is the purpose of the PT1/PT2/PT3/PT4 wiring harnesses marked with red arrows in the image? I see that the schematic notes PT = PASSTHROUGH WIRES. My question is: why is this “PASSTHROUGH WIRES” method necessary?
2: Does “CAN0 - CAR MAIN” mean that this should be connected to the vehicle's original IPMA harness?
3: What does “CAN2 - CAMERA MAIN” refer to?

{Attachments}
https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/1282121586002104402/1494030787195175105/image.png?ex=6a625319&is=6a610199&hm=bba5f600d7351dc59d881f72d015c4fa009c605df04b42dd5e8bd51622c8b61d&


[4/15/2026 1:35 PM] jacobwaller
so passthroughs are necessary most of the time. They might be unused by the comma, but the car may need them.

Can0 and can2 are the "same" bus. It's just that we split them logically. Can2 goes towards the camera, can0 goes towards the car


[4/15/2026 2:02 PM] alvazz
Man, you're amazing,you cleared up my confusion in an instant.
I have a whole new understanding of this. Could you verify if my thinking is correct?
My idea: Originally, the IPMA connector on the windshield was connected to the vehicle's own harness, but in order to access certain vehicle data (CAN 0, CAN 1, CAN 2), we created a custom OpenPilot harness. Simply put, it’s a 1-to-2 splitter: we plug the custom female connector into the IPMA slot, and then plug the original vehicle’s female connector into the OpenPilot harness’s male connector. This way, we ensure both the original vehicle’s electrical connectivity and that OpenPilot receives the necessary data. Is that correct?


[4/15/2026 2:20 PM] jacobwaller
Yes, note that we do split 0 and 2. That's crucial because it allows us to block messages on that bus and also send our own


[4/15/2026 2:20 PM] jacobwaller
the comma has 3 buses. 0/2 as described and also 1, which is used for monitoring. We can send messages on 1, but we cannot block them


[4/15/2026 2:39 PM] alvazz
This raises a new question: I understand that the “Bus 0” and “Bus 2” you mentioned refer to CAN0 - CAR MAIN and CAN2 - CAMERA MAIN, but isn't “Bus 1” the radar?

{Attachments}
https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/1282121586002104402/1494059689124692150/image.png?ex=6a626e04&is=6a611c84&hm=d9f1fde60367422ad6ed6c286bbbb2889c17662e3ec0d97f20d5282f5a777893&


[4/15/2026 3:52 PM] inertiaimpact
> it’s a 1-to-2 splitter:

Kinda but no - it's not just a Y splitter.  Some of the PT wires do go from [camera -> car] but some do go [camera -> harness box in] then a separate [harness box out -> car]


[4/15/2026 3:56 PM] jacobwaller
it can be, but it's really just any other bus we may want to read from


[4/15/2026 10:58 PM] alvazz
oh, got it

[4/23/2026 2:11 AM] mesoplz
Thanks for this! 

Seeing this gave me the inspiration to solve t a different way. I added a low pass filter to snuff out the high frequency jitters at low speed. This feels a LOT better than just having a deadzone. Because when the model decides to move the wheel more than the deadzone, the wheel jerks to the desired angle.

Here's the code if you're interested :)

https://github.com/RWL-Dittrich/opendbc/blob/psa-sunny/opendbc/car/psa/carcontroller.py

I also added some smoothing to the engagement which will (over the course of 1 second) gradually move the wheel to the desired angle that openpilot wants it to be at instead of jerking the wheel harshly to that spot. 

All in all those two changes really improve the driver feel imo. I also had @semitop7 test it in his car. He said it "works really well, even on a cobblestone road".

I'm wondering though, is having these two "filters" inside the car port in OpenDBC the right spot? What do you guys think? Or does this kind of filtering have to live somewhere else in the codebase?

https://github.com/RWL-Dittrich/opendbc/blob/psa-sunny/opendbc/car/psa/carcontroller.py

[5/19/2026 8:29 PM] mvl3c
Any thoughts on preferred style in comma opendbc to code a car complexity?   I have a car model that seems to use different EPS with different canbus messaging per trim level.   The message that gets all the carstate steering info starts one byte off between trim levels, and overlaps.

Option 3 / Any other ideas?  eg: can a dynamic DBC be assigned to the same car model in values.py?

openpilot runtime not receiving new messages I added to dbc *after relay opens*

it must be something obvious, but what am I missing here?
 * I've added a new message to the dbc
 * the car code can reference the new message and signals fine
 * signals can be read at runtime before the harness relay opens
 * **after relay opens, the new mesasge is no longer seen** (canparser timeout)
 * confirmed on cabana streaming that message is present at 10Hz

*--> answer: the source of the added message is before the relay, but carstate was reading from after the relay*


[5/25/2026 6:58 AM] blade1k
Hi,  Tesla Model 3 2018 (HW2.5/AP2) — standstill start via CAN                                                                                                                                           
  
  We're building a system to control a Tesla Model 3 2018 (HW2.5/AP2) via CAN from a laptop, using a Red Panda + Tesla 3-CAN harness. The end goal is fully programmatic control: the car should be able to start from a complete
  standstill, accelerate, brake and steer — all via CAN commands with no driver input on pedals or stalk (except a one-time ACC activation if unavoidable).
  
  What works: Steering via DAS_steeringControl ✓
  
  What doesn't work: Longitudinal control:
  - Gas: DAS_longControl (0x209) with DAS_locMode=1, DAS_locState=0, DAS_locRequest=1/3, DAS_locAccelMin/Max, DAS_locSpeed, Tesla AUTOSAR CRC
  - Brake: ECU_BrakeCommand (0x553), CRC-8 poly 0x11d
  
  Key problem: Pulling TACC stalk from standstill — DI_cruiseState stays UNAVAILABLE. ACC only engaged once at ~40 kph. Even with cruise=ENABLED at speed and tx_blocked=0, long_active=1 — no gas/brake response.

  Questions:
  1. Is DAS_longControl only accepted when TACC is ENABLED? Or can DI respond independently?
  2. Can ACC engage from a complete standstill on HW2.5, or is minimum speed required?
  3. Any known issues with DAS_locMode/State/Request values on AP2?
  4. Could a pedal interceptor (comma pedal or similar) be a better path for standstill start on HW2.5? Which one is compatible with Model 3 2018?

[6/7/2026 9:26 PM] sunnyhaibin
@adeeb in sunnypilot we have a few RX check messages that are 1Hz from the cars. The minimum frequency enforcement from [this #3359 PR](https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/3359) currently breaks those cars due to `safetyRxChecksInvalid` since they can't meet the 10Hz floor.

Would you be open to [this #3445 PR](https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/3445) with an optional flag on `CanMsgCheck` so these messages can opt out of the minimum check while still going through other validations?

{Embed}

[6/16/2026 5:08 PM] davidcaliforniaa
If anyone is interested in adding a WIP car to aliceinforkland.com(hopefully coming soon) I will be using opendbc-community-data as the home for the data. The [readme](https://github.com/ugtthis/opendbc-community-data/blob/main/README.md) goes into how, but for WIP cars its adding an entry into `<make>.toml`. Example entry below

```
[[cars]]
# required
make = "Tesla"
model = "Cybertruck 2077 (with hw10)"
hardware_needed = "comma X"
supported_package = "All"
acc = "openpilot"
no_acc_below = "0 mph"
no_alc_below = "0 mph"
auto_resume_available = true
branch_name = "the-cybertruck"
branch_url = "https://github.com/elon/openpilot/tree/the-cybertruck"
branch_desc = "Highly experimental branch with limited validation."
wiki_url = "https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/wiki/Tesla"
discord_url = "https://discord.com/channels/469524606043160576/524328474081755137"
discord_name = "elon"

# optional
extra_resource_url = ""
video = ""
setup_video = ""
important_notes = [
  "Current support is confirmed only for models manufactured after March 2077",
  "Further tuning is still needed for rough-road steering behavior.",
]
```

[6/26/2026 6:15 PM] badrmoujane
Hi everyone,🫡 

I’m reverse engineering the CAN bus on a Nissan X-Trail/Qashqai T32 (2017-2020) instrument cluster.

I already identified the engine RPM message from the ECU:

* CAN ID 0x1F9 (505 decimal) → ENGINE_1 → RPM

This matches the OpenDBC definition and behaves exactly as expected.

However, I’m trying to identify the CAN message that the instrument cluster actually uses to move the RPM needle, not the raw ECU RPM message.

During logging I found these messages continuously present:

* 0x292
* 0x299
* 0x2D3

These IDs are not present in the OpenDBC Nissan X-Trail DBC, yet they seem related to the combination meter.

My question is:

* Does the cluster receive the RPM directly from 0x1F9, or is it translated by the Gateway/Combination Meter into another CAN message?
* Has anyone identified the CAN ID responsible for driving the tachometer needle?
* Are 0x292, 0x299, or 0x2D3 known cluster/internal messages?

Any CAN dumps, DBC information, or reverse engineering notes would be greatly appreciated.

Thanks!

[7/10/2026 8:30 AM] tealv2
opendbc contains DBCs and CAN logic used by openpilot for lateral and longitudinal actuation. Cabana is a CAN data viewer / grapher, and uses DBCs to show data neatly defined. Cabana creates DBCs and you can save them by clicking file and save DBC

{Attachments}
https://cdn.discordapp.com/attachments/1282121586002104402/1525132056252907590/IMG_0773.png?ex=6a621769&is=6a60c5e9&hm=6054f4e78eb9a6f124c7a1caf2446d6377e484c06fb685ceae861f9d73f68882&

[7/21/2026 5:54 PM] sunnyhaibin
@adeeb @shane In this PR there's a comment of "they're all essentially the same... this can't make a difference": https://github.com/commaai/openpilot/pull/38394 and https://github.com/commaai/opendbc/pull/3572

For HKG we find cars require different values during stopping but the default one (`-0.8`) was borderline acceptable.
